    • Concept introduced, POST-as-upsert (BR-107). [Rubric §9, API & Contract Design] assesses how idempotent write semantics are expressed to clients. SubmitAnswerAsync (IFeedbackUIService.cs:23-27) is one method for both the first answer and every edit: the server upserts on (eventId, questionId) for the caller, so the UI never branches on create-versus-update and never has to know whether an answer already exists. That is what lets EventFeedback retry a partially failed submit by pressing the same button, since re-posting an already-saved answer is a no-op. This is the UI service abstraction of IBookmarkUIService specialized to feedback.
    • Walkthrough (IFeedbackUIService.cs:23-36): SubmitAnswerAsync(eventId, questionId, answerValue, ct) returns the persisted EventQuestionAnswerDTO (:23-27), nullable on a refusal; GetMyAnswersAsync(eventId, ct) returns the caller's answers for one event (:29-31), which is what the page pre-fills from; DeleteAnswerAsync(eventId, answerId, ct) removes one by its server-assigned id (:33-36). Note the answer value crosses as a plain string: rating and text questions share one transport shape and the question type decides how it is read back.
    • Where it's used: implemented by EventFeedbackService, registered scoped (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:35), consumed by EventFeedback.

    INowNextService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/INowNextService.cs:38 · Level 2 · interface

    • What it is: the UI-side contract for reading one event's now-and-next snapshot, a single method returning a nullable NowNextSnapshot.
    • Depends on: NowNextSnapshot and the EventIdentifierType alias. Externals: BCL Task, CancellationToken.
    • Concept, a port over a server-owned computation. [Rubric §18, UI Architecture] assesses whether components depend on typed contracts rather than transport, and [Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness] whether cross-module reads travel through an explicit boundary. The interface doc (INowNextService.cs:32-37) is the contract worth memorizing, because it enumerates what the server owns and the client therefore must not re-derive: session eligibility (scheduled, non-service, not declined or cancelled), the event-local wall-clock conversion, and the "next = the batch sharing the earliest future start" rule. Concretely, eligibility is CalendarExportMapper.IsExportable (GetNowNextHandler.cs:54), which needs both StartsAt and EndsAt set, IsServiceSession false, and the status inside the BR-49 allow-list. None of that is visible in a public catalog listing, which is precisely why the page cannot compute now-and-next itself; the implementation's doc records that this endpoint "replaces the old whole-catalog fetch on Happening Now" (NowNextService.cs:8-11).
    • Walkthrough (INowNextService.cs:46-48): one method, GetAsync(eventId, cancellationToken = default), returning Task<NowNextSnapshot?>. The nullable return is the whole error contract: null means the event was not found or is not published (doc at :40-43), which the server produces as a Result.Failure carrying Error.NotFound when the event is missing or IsPublished is false (GetNowNextHandler.cs:32-36) and the API surfaces as a 404, translated back to null by the implementation (NowNextService.cs:27-28). Returning null rather than throwing is what lets a caller degrade to an empty schedule with no try/catch of its own.
    • Why it's built this way: the one-method port is the DI swap point (services.AddScoped<INowNextService, NowNextService>(), MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:53) and the test extension point, so the page can be exercised against a fake snapshot with no HTTP stack; it also keeps Engagement free of any compile-time reference to Conference for this read (ADR-007 and ADR-008 put the module in its own process, so this crosses a real service boundary over public HTTP; the implementation uses the plain "APIClient" factory client with no token, NowNextService.cs:20, because the endpoint is anonymous).
    • Where it's used: injected into the HappeningNow page (HappeningNow.razor.cs:27) and called once per load from LoadSessionsAsync (:183). Implemented by NowNextService; covered by NowNextServiceTests.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: the contract exposes only the per-event form. The endpoint also serves an id-less form where the server auto-selects the current-or-next published event, but no first-party C# client for it exists in this UI; the Android widget calls Events/now-next with a bare HttpClient instead (NowNextWidgetProvider.cs:119-120). Note also a wording gap: the doc comment says "not declined or cancelled" while the code's actual gate is the export allow-list above.

    IQuestionLookupService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/IFeedbackUIService.cs:10 · Level 2 · interface

    • What it is: a small read contract that loads the feedback questions to render, filtered by whether they apply to an Event or a Session.
    • Depends on: QuestionDTO, a Conference-owned contract (IFeedbackUIService.cs:2). Externals: BCL Task.
    • Concept, the cross-module UI read. [Rubric §18, UI Architecture] and [Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness]. Feedback lives in Engagement but the questions are Conference-owned, so this contract lets the Engagement UI pull them through the same authenticated HTTP layer without a compile-time reference to Conference internals: only the shared QuestionDTO crosses the boundary. This is a different trade-off from the mirrored shape on NowNextSessionInfo, and the difference is worth noticing: a .Shared DTO is a published contract project both sides may reference, while a mirror is what you write when you do not want the reference at all.
    • Walkthrough (IFeedbackUIService.cs:12-14): one method, GetQuestionsAsync(questionEntity, ct), where questionEntity is the string discriminator ("Event" or "Session"), returning a read-only list of QuestionDTO. The discriminator is a bare string rather than an enum, so a typo at a call site is a runtime empty list, not a compile error.
    • Where it's used: implemented by QuestionLookupService, registered scoped (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:34), consumed by both EventFeedback and SessionFeedback to build their question lists.

    ISessionFeedbackUIService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/IFeedbackUIService.cs:43 · Level 2 · interface

    • What it is: the session counterpart of IEventFeedbackUIService: submit, read and delete a user's own answers to session feedback questions. Structurally identical, scoped to a session id instead of an event id.
    • Depends on: SessionQuestionAnswerDTO and the SessionIdentifierType / QuestionIdentifierType / SessionQuestionAnswerIdentifierType aliases.
    • Concept: the POST-as-upsert semantics (BR-107) are taught on IEventFeedbackUIService and apply here unchanged (IFeedbackUIService.cs:39-42).
    • Walkthrough (IFeedbackUIService.cs:45-58): SubmitAnswerAsync(sessionId, questionId, answerValue, ct) (:45-49), GetMyAnswersAsync(sessionId, ct) (:51-53), DeleteAnswerAsync(sessionId, answerId, ct) (:55-58). The only differences from the event twin are the scoping identifier and the answer DTO; the two interfaces are kept as separate types rather than one generic contract because the answer DTOs and the identifier aliases differ, and a shared generic would buy nothing at three methods apiece.

    SessionReminderCoordinator

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/SessionReminderCoordinator.cs:16 · Level 2 · sealed partial class

    • What it is: the stateful orchestrator that keeps on-device session reminders in sync with the user's bookmarks. It holds the tracked-session set and the reminder settings in device preferences, replans through the pure SessionReminderPlanner, and cancel-then-reschedules the difference through the platform notification service.
    • Depends on: ILocalNotificationService, IDevicePreferences, ISessionLookupService, ILiveEventUIService, SessionReminderPlanner, SessionReminder, LocalNotificationRequest. Externals: IStringLocalizer<T>, ILogger<T>.
    • Concept introduced, best-effort client-side state sync. [Rubric §19, State Management] and [Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity]. The authoritative reminder state is the tracked set persisted in IDevicePreferences; every public entry point mutates it and then recomputes the whole plan, which self-heals drift from changes made on another device. Crucially, reminders are a convenience: every path is wrapped so a failure logs and returns rather than failing the bookmark operation that triggered it (SessionReminderCoordinator.cs:11-14), and on hosts without local notifications (the web heads) the whole class no-ops behind the IsSupported guard. [Rubric §27, Internationalization]: the notification title and body come from the injected IStringLocalizer (:173-174), never from a hardcoded string.
    • Walkthrough:
      • Constants (:25-33): the public preference keys EnabledKey and LeadMinutesKey, the private TrackedSessionsKey, and DefaultLeadMinutes = 15.
      • IsSupported (:36) delegates to the notification service so a settings UI can hide reminder controls entirely where reminders are impossible.
      • NotifyBookmarkAddedAsync (:39-40) and NotifyBookmarkRemovedAsync (:43-44) both funnel into the private MutateTrackedAsync, appending-distinct or filtering the session id with the alias-safe EqualityComparer<SessionIdentifierType>.Default.
      • ResyncAsync(bookmarkedSessionIds, ct) (:50-51) replaces the tracked set wholesale with an authoritative bookmark map: the drift-healing path.
      • ApplySettingsAsync(enabled, leadMinutes, ct) (:54-72) persists the two settings then replans, inside a try/catch that swallows everything except OperationCanceledException and logs through LogReminderSyncFailed.
      • GetSettingsAsync(ct) (:75-80) reads the current settings back for the settings UI, defaulting to enabled and 15 minutes.
      • MutateTrackedAsync(mutate, ct) (:82-110) is the shared core: guard on IsSupported, load the tracked set, apply the mutation, persist it, cancel the notifications for ids that left the set (:97-102), then ReplanAsync.
      • ReplanAsync(tracked, ct) (:112-179) is where the ordering matters. It short-circuits when reminders are off or nothing is tracked, cancelling everything only in the "off" case (:114-124); requests OS permission, which the comment notes appears at most once and silently leaves reminders off on a denial (:126-131); resolves the current live event and returns if there is none (:133-137); filters the tracked sessions to the ones the lookup knows and that belong to that event (:141-145); and calls SessionReminderPlanner.Plan with DateTimeOffset.UtcNow (:147-151). Then the stale sweep (:153-167): a session can stay tracked and still drop out of the plan (deleted, moved to another event, times cleared per BR-16, or its start moved into the past), nothing upstream cancels those, so the tracked ids are diffed against the plan and the difference cancelled. The comment is explicit that this runs only after a plan was actually computed, because cancelling on a transient null live event would wipe reminders that are still valid. Finally each SessionReminder becomes a LocalNotificationRequest and is scheduled (:169-178).
      • LogReminderSyncFailed (:181-182) is a source-generated [LoggerMessage] at Warning. [Rubric §13, Observability & Operability]: structured logging with no allocation on the happy path, and a message that says what the operator loses (reminders may be stale until the next bookmark change) rather than just that something failed.
    • Why it's built this way: pushing all the calendar math into the pure planner leaves this class a thin, resilient adapter over device capabilities, and the cancel-diff-then-reschedule approach relies on the planner's stable SessionReminder.NotificationId so rescheduling never duplicates (ADR-042 Wave 2). It is registered as a concrete scoped type with no interface (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:31), which is the honest shape here: it has exactly one implementation and its own dependencies are already the swappable extension points.
    • Where it's used: injected into SessionBookmarkUIService (SessionBookmarkUIService.cs:20), which calls ResyncAsync after an authoritative bookmark read (:46), NotifyBookmarkAddedAsync after a create (:73) and NotifyBookmarkRemovedAsync after a delete (:100), so reminders track bookmarks no matter which page toggled them. The MAUI device-settings page reads and writes the settings (MMCA.ADC/Source/Hosts/UI/MMCA.ADC.UI/Pages/DeviceSettings.razor:4, :61, :67, :89, :95). Covered by SessionReminderCoordinatorTests.

    BookmarkService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/BookmarkService.cs:15 · Level 3 · sealed class

    • What it is: the HTTP implementation of IBookmarkUIService, talking to the bookmarks resource through the Gateway with authenticated, retried requests. The endpoint prefix is the const Endpoint = "bookmarks" (BookmarkService.cs:19).
    • Depends on: IBookmarkUIService (the contract it satisfies), AuthenticatedServiceBase (base class), ITokenStorageService, ServiceExceptionHelper, CreateBookmarkRequest, UserSessionBookmarkDTO, PagedCollectionResult<T> (BookmarkService.cs:5). Externals: IHttpClientFactory, System.Net.Http.Json, System.Globalization, System.Net.
    • Concept introduced, the authenticated HTTP UI service. This is the shape every write-capable service in this module follows, so it is worth reading once in full. Three inherited pieces do the work, all in AuthenticatedServiceBase:
      • CreateAuthenticatedClientAsync() (MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/AuthenticatedServiceBase.cs:57) takes the named "APIClient" from the factory (:59), reads the access token from the circuit-scoped ITokenStorageService and sets the Bearer header (:63-68). An InvalidOperationException is swallowed (:70-73), which is the SSR prerender pass where JS interop is not available yet: the call proceeds unauthenticated rather than throwing. [Rubric §26, Front-End Security] assesses how a client handles credentials; the token is never persisted by this class, it is fetched per call from the storage abstraction (ADR-051).
      • RetryPolicy (AuthenticatedServiceBase.cs:26-32) is a static Polly policy: 3 retries with exponential backoff (2s, 4s, 8s) plus up to one second of jitter so a room full of clients does not re-converge on the same instant. Retryable means 5xx except 501 and 505, plus 408 and 429 (:89-98). [Rubric §29, Resilience & Business Continuity] assesses whether transient failures are absorbed close to where they happen; this is the client half of that.
      • ServiceExceptionHelper.ThrowIfDomainExceptionAsync (MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/Services/ServiceExceptionHelper.cs:17) parses an RFC 9457 problem body and re-throws it as a DomainInvariantViolationException carrying the server's message, so a page can render the original business error instead of a status code. A non-JSON body returns quietly and the caller's EnsureSuccessStatusCode() handles it (ServiceExceptionHelper.cs:33-38). [Rubric §9, API & Contract Design] assesses whether the error contract survives the transport, and this is where it is decoded.
    • Walkthrough (members in call order)
      • CreateAsync (BookmarkService.cs:21-36) POSTs the CreateBookmarkRequest as JSON to bookmarks and deserializes the created UserSessionBookmarkDTO. The failure path is the standard pair: translate a domain problem (:31-32), then EnsureSuccessStatusCode() for anything left (:34).
      • GetUserBookmarksAsync (:38-73) builds its query string by hand into a List<string> with string.Create(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, ...) per parameter (:47-52) and appends eventId only when the optional filter is supplied (:54-55). Invariant formatting is not decoration here: these identifiers are interpolated into a URL, and a culture-sensitive numeric format would produce a query the API cannot bind. The response is read as a PagedCollectionResult<T> and flattened into a (Items, TotalItems) tuple with PaginationMetadata.TotalItemCount (:67-72), defaulting to an empty list and zero.
      • DeleteAsync (:75-93) maps a 404 to false (:85-86) before the domain-exception translation, so "already gone" is an ordinary answer rather than an exception, and returns true on success.
    • Why it's built this way: the class doc (:11-14) states it directly, the bookmark endpoints (create with an owner in the body, list by user, delete by bookmark id) do not fit the generic EntityServiceBase CRUD shape, so this service spells out the three calls the BookmarksController contract actually offers (BookmarksController.cs:43, :76, :115).
    • Where it's used: registered scoped as IBookmarkUIService in the module's UI wiring (MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:26). [Rubric §14, Testability]: covered directly by BookmarkServiceTests (MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Tests/Services/BookmarkServiceTests.cs).
    • Caveats / not-in-source: no page injects IBookmarkUIService today. The only references in the repo are the interface, this implementation and the DI registration; the inline bookmark toggles the UI actually renders go through SessionBookmarkUIService instead. Note also that CreateAsync sends no Idempotency-Key header, unlike the live-layer services in this module (LivePollUIService.cs:96), so a retried create relies on the server's duplicate guard rather than on idempotency replay. Finally, the three calls use the retry overload without a CancellationToken (:27, :59, :82): the token still reaches the HTTP call itself, but Polly's own backoff waits are not cancellable on this path (contrast SessionFeedbackService, which passes it).

    CheckInService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/CheckInService.cs:15 · Level 3 · sealed class

    • What it is: the single HTTP client for every badge and scan surface in the module, implementing ICheckInUIService against the checkins resource (CheckInService.cs:19). It carries six calls with three different audiences: the attendee reading their own badge, the organizer scanning or manually checking someone in, and the attendee self-recording a sponsor booth or room visit.
    • Depends on: ICheckInUIService, AuthenticatedServiceBase, ITokenStorageService, ServiceExceptionHelper, MyBadgeDTO, CheckInAttendeeRequest, ManualCheckInRequest, CheckInResultDTO, SponsorVisitRequest, SponsorVisitResultDTO, RoomCheckInRequest, RoomCheckInResultDTO, AttendanceStatsDTO, SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>. Externals: IHttpClientFactory, System.Text.Json (JsonDocument), System.Net.Http.Json, System.Globalization, System.Net.
    • Concept introduced, two failure dialects in one client. The authenticated-HTTP shape is taught at BookmarkService; what is new here is that this class deliberately reports failure two different ways depending on who is looking at the screen. [Rubric §9, API & Contract Design] assesses whether a client contract preserves the distinctions the server actually makes. The organizer calls throw (a failed scan is a fault an operator should see reported as one), while the two attendee self-service calls never throw on a refusal: they return a SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult> carrying the server's stable error code, because the QR landing pages have to tell "nothing is scheduled in this room" from "the event is not published" and both arrive as HTTP 404 (CheckInService.cs:113-120). A status code alone cannot pick the state, so the code is read out of the problem body and handed to the page. [Rubric §26, Front-End Security] is the reason the pages then collapse several of those codes into one message: see CheckInState and VisitState.
    • Walkthrough (public calls first, then the two private posters)
      • GetMyBadgeAsync (:22-38) GETs checkins/my-badge and maps a 404 to null (:30-31), which is the "no badge yet" answer the badge page renders as an empty state. The server mints a badge on first use, so this read is a create-or-get on the other side (CheckInsController.cs:45).
      • CheckInByCredentialAsync (:41-44) and ManualCheckInAsync (:47-50) are one-line delegations to PostCheckInAsync, differing only in the relative URL: the root checkins and checkins/manual. Both server endpoints are permission-gated on EngagementPermissions.CheckInManage (CheckInsController.cs:68, :88), so the client does no role check of its own.
      • RecordSponsorVisitAsync (:53-59) and RecordRoomCheckInAsync (:62-68) build their one-field requests inline (SponsorVisitRequest, RoomCheckInRequest) and delegate to PostSelfCheckInAsync. Neither call passes an attendee identity: the server takes it from the token, so a scanned code can only ever check in the person holding the phone.
      • GetAttendanceStatsAsync (:71-88) GETs checkins/stats?eventId= with invariant formatting (:77) and returns the AttendanceStatsDTO rollup; the endpoint is permission-gated the same way as the writes (CheckInsController.cs:157).
      • PostCheckInAsync<TRequest> (:95-111) is the throwing poster shared by the two organizer writes. Its doc comment (:90-94) records why there is no conflict branch: a repeat check-in comes back 200 with AlreadyCheckedIn set rather than 409, so the caller has one success shape to interpret.
      • PostSelfCheckInAsync<TRequest, TResult> (:121-140) is the non-throwing poster. On success it wraps the payload (:133-137); on any non-success it returns new SelfCheckInOutcome<TResult>(null, await ReadErrorCodeAsync(...)) (:139), so a refusal is data, not an exception.
      • ReadErrorCodeAsync (:147-182) walks the problem body's errors array and returns the first element's code string (:158-172). Everything else yields null: an empty body, a body with no errors array, a non-string code, or a JsonException from an HTML error page or a bare challenge (:176-181, with the comment naming that case). null is a meaningful value here, it is what the pages render as the generic "unavailable" state.
    • Why it's built this way: the class doc (:11-14) sets the rule that every call is authenticated, including the badge read, because the badge is caller-scoped rather than public. The split between the two private posters is the client-side consequence of a server design decision recorded in ADR-072: organizer scanning and attendee self-service produce the same domain writes but face very different humans, so the refusal vocabulary is stable (CheckInErrorCodes) and the client passes it through untranslated.
    • Where it's used: registered scoped as ICheckInUIService (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:40) and injected by five surfaces: MyBadge (MyBadge.razor.cs:48), CheckInScan (CheckInScan.razor.cs:159, :191), OrganizerAttendance (OrganizerAttendance.razor.cs:49), RoomCheckIn and SponsorVisit. The whole controller sits behind the EngagementFeatures.CheckIn gate (CheckInsController.cs:32), so a deployment with the feature off answers 404 to every one of these calls.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: there is no CheckInServiceTests file in the UI test project; this class is exercised indirectly through the bUnit page tests (MyBadgeTests, CheckInScanTests, OrganizerAttendanceTests, RoomCheckInTests, SponsorVisitTests), which mock the interface rather than the transport. Which error codes a given server build emits is the API's contract, not this file's.

    EventFeedbackService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/EventFeedbackService.cs:13 · Level 3 · sealed class

    • What it is: the HTTP implementation of IEventFeedbackUIService, backed by the eventquestionanswers resource (EventFeedbackService.cs:17). Its submit call is a POST-as-upsert: the same POST creates a first answer or replaces an existing one (BR-107, per the class doc at :10-11).
    • Depends on: IEventFeedbackUIService, AuthenticatedServiceBase, ITokenStorageService, ServiceExceptionHelper, EventQuestionAnswerDTO, PagedCollectionResult<T>. Externals: IHttpClientFactory, System.Net.Http.Json, System.Globalization.
    • Concept: the authenticated-retried-HTTP shape is taught at BookmarkService. What this pair of feedback services adds is the upsert contract: [Rubric §9, API & Contract Design] assesses whether a client has to know which of create or update applies, and here it does not, which is what lets the feedback page loop over answered questions and post each one the same way regardless of whether the attendee is answering or revising.
    • Walkthrough
      • SubmitAnswerAsync (:19-38) posts an anonymous object { EventId, QuestionId, AnswerValue } (:27) rather than a named request record, and reads back the persisted EventQuestionAnswerDTO.
      • GetMyAnswersAsync (:40-57) builds a filtered paged URL against the generic paged endpoint: filters[EventId].operator=equals&filters[EventId].value={eventId}&pageSize=100&includeChildren=false (:46). Note what it does not do: there is no user filter in the query, because the endpoint scopes to the caller server-side. It goes straight to EnsureSuccessStatusCode() (:51) without the domain-exception translation, since a read has no business-rule failure body to decode.
      • DeleteAnswerAsync (:59-74) DELETEs by answer id with the owning eventId as a query parameter (:66), and translates a domain failure before EnsureSuccessStatusCode().
    • Where it's used: registered scoped as IEventFeedbackUIService (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:35); injected by EventFeedback (EventFeedback.razor.cs:19), which is the only consumer. Covered by EventFeedbackServiceTests (MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Tests/Services/EventFeedbackServiceTests.cs).
    • Caveats / not-in-source: all three calls use the retry overload without a CancellationToken (:29, :48, :67), so Polly's backoff waits are not cancellable here even though the token reaches the HTTP call; its session-scoped twin SessionFeedbackService passes the token. The pageSize=100 on the read is a hard ceiling in this file: an event with more than 100 answers by one attendee would be truncated silently, which no page currently approaches but nothing here guards.

    IPointsUIService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/IPointsUIService.cs:9 · Level 3 · interface

    • What it is: the UI-side contract for the points game: the attendee's own ledger, the leaderboard opt-in toggle, the public leaderboard, and the organizer rollup. Four members, one per surface (IPointsUIService.cs:9-46).
    • Depends on: MyPointsDTO, LeaderboardEntryDTO, PointsOverviewDTO (all from MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Shared.Points, :1). No BCL dependency beyond Task and CancellationToken.
    • Concept introduced, the identity-free client contract. [Rubric §11, Security] assesses whether authorization can be influenced from the client, and this interface is a good example of designing that risk away: not one member takes a user identifier. GetMyPointsAsync (:18-21) takes only paging, and SetLeaderboardParticipationAsync (:36) takes only a boolean, with the doc comment stating that the published name is read from the caller's token server-side and never from the call (:31-33). A client that cannot name a subject cannot ask for the wrong one. [Rubric §30, Compliance, Privacy & Data Governance] is the other half: the leaderboard is opt-in, so publishing a name is an explicit act with its own endpoint rather than a side effect of earning points. [Rubric §9, API & Contract Design] shows in the defaults: paging defaults are declared once here (pageNumber = 1, pageSize = 20 at :19-20, recentCount = 20 at :44) and match the server's own defaults (PointsController.cs:54-55, :126).
    • Walkthrough: GetMyPointsAsync (:18-21) returns a nullable MyPointsDTO carrying the running total, the opt-in state and one page of awarded entries, newest first. GetLeaderboardAsync (:28) returns the public list, non-nullable, so "nobody has opted in" is an empty list rather than null. SetLeaderboardParticipationAsync (:36) returns a bare Task: the server answers 204, so there is nothing to hand back and the caller re-reads its own points to see the new state. GetPointsOverviewAsync (:43-45) returns the organizer rollup and is documented as organizer only (:39), enforced server-side by EngagementPermissions.PointsViewOverview (PointsController.cs:121), not here.
    • Why it's built this way: keeping the interface in the UI layer, over Shared DTOs only, is what lets a page be bUnit-tested against a mock with no HTTP in sight, and lets the MAUI head and the web head resolve different implementations if they ever need to. [Rubric §1, SOLID]: the four members are the four things the pages do, no more, so no consumer depends on a method it does not call.
    • Where it's used: implemented by PointsService and registered scoped (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:44). Injected by MyPoints (MyPoints.razor.cs:19) and OrganizerPointsOverview (OrganizerPointsOverview.razor.cs:23); both page test classes mock this interface (MyPointsTests, OrganizerPointsOverviewTests).

    NowNextService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/NowNextService.cs:13 · Level 3 · sealed class

    • What it is: the HTTP implementation of INowNextService, one GET against the Conference API's anonymous Events/{id}/now-next endpoint, deserialized into a NowNextSnapshot and handed back untouched.
    • Depends on: INowNextService, NowNextSnapshot. Externals: IHttpClientFactory, HttpClient / HttpStatusCode, System.Net.Http.Json's ReadFromJsonAsync, CultureInfo.
    • Concept introduced, the thin lookup service (the contrast with the authenticated services above). [Rubric §18, UI Architecture] and [Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability]. Unlike BookmarkService this class does not derive from AuthenticatedServiceBase: it takes the named "APIClient" from the factory directly (NowNextService.cs:20), the same shape as the module's other read-only lookups (SessionLookupService). That named client is registered once in the framework (MMCA.Common/Source/Presentation/MMCA.Common.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:63) with the gateway base address plus the auth and culture delegating handlers, so a bearer token still rides along when the user has one, but the endpoint is [AllowAnonymous] (MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.API/Controllers/EventsController.cs:224) and an anonymous visitor gets the same payload. The consequence to notice: no token fetch and no RetryPolicy wrap this call, because there is nothing user-specific to authorize and the answer is cheap to re-request.
    • Walkthrough (NowNextService.cs:16-33), which is mostly guards
      • Both disposables are scoped with using, the client (:20) and the response (:22), so a per-call fetch leaks neither.
      • The relative URI is built with string.Create(CultureInfo.InvariantCulture, $"Events/{eventId}/now-next") (:23). Invariant formatting is not decoration: the id is interpolated into a path, and a culture-sensitive numeric format would produce a path the API cannot route.
      • 404 degrades to null (:27-28), with the comment naming the case it covers (an unpublished or deleted event) and the consequence (the page shows nothing scheduled). This is the branch that satisfies the interface's nullable contract, and it is checked before any success handling.
      • Every other non-success throws via EnsureSuccessStatusCode() (:30). A 5xx is therefore not silently swallowed: it propagates to the page's catch, which sets a localized load error. The split is deliberate, "no such event" is a normal outcome, "the service is broken" is not.
      • The body is read with ReadFromJsonAsync<NowNextSnapshot>(cancellationToken) (:32) and returned as is, so a literal JSON null body also yields null and lands on the same empty-state path as a 404.
      • The CancellationToken is threaded through both the GET (:24) and the read (:32), so page disposal cancels an in-flight fetch.
      • There is no client-side filtering, sorting or time math anywhere in the method: the lists arrive ordered and filtered and are passed straight to the page.
    • Why it's built this way: the class doc (:7-12) states the rationale directly, the endpoint already excludes service, declined and cancelled sessions and does the event-local time math, so nothing is recomputed here. The previous shape (a whole-catalog fetch plus client-side time comparison) could not tell an ineligible session apart, since eligibility is not part of a public catalog listing. The call is also cheap to repeat: the server output-caches it under the NowNextCache policy (EventsController.cs:225) and the payload is identical for every role, so no per-user cache key is needed.
    • Where it's used: registered scoped as INowNextService in the module's UI wiring (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:53); consumed only by the HappeningNow page today (HappeningNow.razor.cs:27, :183). [Rubric §14, Testability]: covered by three facts in NowNextServiceTests, the exact request path (:19), the unchanged projection of the server snapshot (:32), and the 404-to-null degradation (:48).
    • Caveats / not-in-source: no timeout, retry or circuit breaker is configured on this path in the framework registration of the named client. A transient failure is therefore one exception and one localized error message, with a page reload as the only retry.

    QuestionLookupService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/QuestionLookupService.cs:12 · Level 3 · sealed class

    • What it is: the HTTP implementation of IQuestionLookupService. It fetches the user-authored feedback questions for one entity type ("Event" or "Session") from the Conference-owned questions resource.
    • Depends on: IQuestionLookupService, AuthenticatedServiceBase, ITokenStorageService, QuestionDTO, PagedCollectionResult<T>. Externals: IHttpClientFactory, System.Net.Http.Json.
    • Concept: the same authenticated-HTTP shape as BookmarkService, used here to reach a Conference-owned read endpoint from the Engagement UI. [Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness] assesses whether a module reaches across a boundary through a contract that survives extraction: this one already does, because the call is plain HTTP through the Gateway rather than a project reference into Conference. The Engagement UI references only QuestionDTO from MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared (QuestionLookupService.cs:2), which is the wire contract, not the implementation.
    • Walkthrough (:16-34): GetQuestionsAsync(questionEntity, ct) builds one filtered, paged URL (:22) that constrains QuestionEntity to the URL-escaped argument and QuestionSource to User, with pageSize=100. The Uri.EscapeDataString on the caller-supplied value is the one input-handling detail worth naming: the argument is a string, not an enum, so escaping it is what keeps a stray character from reshaping the query. The retry is the token-aware overload (:24-26), the response goes straight to EnsureSuccessStatusCode() (:28) with no domain-exception translation (a read has no business-failure body), and the items are returned as an IReadOnlyList defaulting to empty (:33).
    • Why it's built this way: filtering QuestionSource to User server-side rather than fetching everything and filtering in the page keeps system-authored questions off the wire entirely, and pushing QuestionEntity into the query lets the same service serve both feedback pages with no branching.
    • Where it's used: registered scoped as IQuestionLookupService (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:34); injected by both feedback pages, EventFeedback (EventFeedback.razor.cs:18) and SessionFeedback (SessionFeedback.razor.cs:18). Covered by QuestionLookupServiceTests (MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Tests/Services/QuestionLookupServiceTests.cs).
    • Caveats / not-in-source: questionEntity is an unconstrained string in both the interface and this implementation, so the valid values ("Event", "Session") are a convention the callers hold, not a type the compiler checks. The pageSize=100 is a hard ceiling in this file.

    SessionBookmarkUIService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/SessionBookmarkUIService.cs:17 · Level 3 · sealed class

    • What it is: a cross-module HTTP service implementing ISessionBookmarkUIService, used by the Conference UI to read bookmark state and toggle bookmarks inline. Beyond the three HTTP calls, every successful read or mutation also feeds the session-reminder layer.
    • Depends on: ISessionBookmarkUIService, AuthenticatedServiceBase, ITokenStorageService, SessionReminderCoordinator (injected as a concrete class, :20), ServiceExceptionHelper, CreateBookmarkRequest, UserSessionBookmarkDTO. Externals: IHttpClientFactory, System.Net.Http.Json, System.Globalization, System.Net.
    • Concept introduced, the side effect on the write path. [Rubric §19, State Management] assesses where derived client state is kept in step with the server. Local session reminders are exactly that kind of derived state: they are only correct if they track the bookmark set, and the bookmark set can change from any page or any device. Putting the reminder calls inside this one service, rather than in each component that toggles a bookmark, is what makes that hold regardless of entry point. [Rubric §29, Resilience]: the reminder calls are best effort by contract, so a reminder failure never surfaces to the bookmark caller (class doc, :13-15). The ordering is the load-bearing part: HTTP first, reminder only after a confirmed success.
    • Walkthrough
      • GetBookmarkedSessionIdsAsync (:25-50) GETs bookmarks/session-ids?userId= and deserializes a Dictionary<SessionIdentifierType, UserSessionBookmarkIdentifierType> (:40-41), which is why a page can render a bookmark toggle and know the id needed to delete it from one call. On a non-null result it calls reminderCoordinator.ResyncAsync([.. result.Keys], cancellationToken) (:44-47) with the comment naming the intent: this response is the authoritative set, so it is the right moment to heal drift caused by another device.
      • CreateBookmarkAsync (:53-77) builds a CreateBookmarkRequest from the two identifiers (:59), POSTs it, and calls NotifyBookmarkAddedAsync(sessionId, ...) only when a non-null DTO came back (:71-74).
      • DeleteBookmarkAsync (:80-102) maps 404 to false before the domain-exception translation (:92-93), and calls NotifyBookmarkRemovedAsync(sessionId, ...) only on a confirmed delete (:100). The 404 path deliberately does not notify: nothing was removed.
      • The coordinator members these three calls use are NotifyBookmarkAddedAsync (SessionReminderCoordinator.cs:39), NotifyBookmarkRemovedAsync (:43) and ResyncAsync (:50).
    • Why it's built this way: the class doc (:10-16) records both halves, the reminder wiring lives here so reminders track bookmarks no matter which page toggled them (ADR-042 Wave 2), and reminder failures never reach the bookmark caller. The coordinator is registered scoped alongside this service (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:31) and its comment there states it is a no-op on hosts whose local-notification capability is unsupported, which is why a web-only host pays nothing for this coupling.
    • Where it's used: registered scoped as ISessionBookmarkUIService (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:27); resolved optionally by the Conference public pages, which call ServiceProvider.GetService<ISessionBookmarkUIService>() and null-check the result (PublicSessionList at PublicSessionList.razor.cs:114, :218; PublicSessionDetail at PublicSessionDetail.razor.cs:52, :183, :204). That optional resolution is the extraction story in practice: Conference renders correctly in a host where the Engagement UI module is not registered at all. Covered by SessionBookmarkUIServiceTests (MMCA.ADC/Tests/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Tests/Services/SessionBookmarkUIServiceTests.cs).
    • Caveats / not-in-source: the dependency is on the concrete SessionReminderCoordinator, not an interface, so a test of this service gets the real coordinator (which is itself a no-op without a supported local-notification capability). Whether a reminder is actually scheduled is decided inside the coordinator and SessionReminderPlanner, not here.

    SessionFeedbackService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/SessionFeedbackService.cs:13 · Level 3 · sealed class

    • What it is: the HTTP implementation of ISessionFeedbackUIService, backed by the sessionquestionanswers resource (:17). The session-scoped twin of EventFeedbackService, same POST-as-upsert contract (BR-107, class doc :10-11).
    • Depends on: ISessionFeedbackUIService, AuthenticatedServiceBase, ITokenStorageService, ServiceExceptionHelper, SessionQuestionAnswerDTO, PagedCollectionResult<T>. Externals: IHttpClientFactory, System.Net.Http.Json, System.Globalization.

SubmitAnswerAsync (:19-39) POSTs the anonymous { SessionId, QuestionId, AnswerValue } object (:27); GetMyAnswersAsync (:41-59) reads the filtered paged list with pageSize=100&includeChildren=false (:47); DeleteAnswerAsync (:61-77) DELETEs by answer id with sessionId as a query parameter (:68). The one mechanical difference from EventFeedbackService worth pointing at: all three calls here use the retry overload that takes a CancellationToken (:29-32, :49-52, :69-71), so Polly's backoff waits are cancellable on this path and are not on the event path. [Rubric §16, Maintainability]: two files this close should not diverge on a detail like that, and the divergence is visible only by reading them side by side.

    PointsService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/PointsService.cs:14 · Level 4 · sealed class

    • What it is: the HTTP implementation of IPointsUIService against the points resource (PointsService.cs:18). Four calls, four endpoints, no client-side computation of any kind.
    • Depends on: IPointsUIService, AuthenticatedServiceBase, ITokenStorageService, ServiceExceptionHelper, MyPointsDTO, LeaderboardEntryDTO, SetLeaderboardParticipationRequest, PointsOverviewDTO. Externals: IHttpClientFactory, System.Net.Http.Json, System.Globalization.
    • Concept: the authenticated-HTTP shape from BookmarkService, applied to a surface where authentication is doing four different jobs at once. The class doc (:9-13) enumerates them, and they are worth reading as a [Rubric §11, Security] exercise: the ledger read and the opt-in are caller-scoped ("my points"), the leaderboard read is caller-agnostic but still requires a signed-in attendee, and the overview is role-gated on the server. The client enforces none of that and does not try to: it attaches the token and lets the server decide, which is why a 403 arrives as a translated domain exception rather than as a page that guessed wrong about permissions.
    • Walkthrough (in interface order)
      • GetMyPointsAsync (:21-39) GETs points/me?pageNumber=&pageSize= with invariant formatting (:28) and returns a nullable MyPointsDTO. No user id is sent; the server reads the caller from the token (PointsController.cs:45-47).
      • GetLeaderboardAsync (:42-57) GETs points/leaderboard and deserializes a List<LeaderboardEntryDTO>, collapsing a null body to an empty list (:55-56) so the page never has to null-check a collection.
      • SetLeaderboardParticipationAsync (:60-79) PUTs a SetLeaderboardParticipationRequest carrying only the boolean (:66) to points/me/leaderboard-participation. The inline comment (:64-65) explains the shape of the round trip: the endpoint answers 204 with no body (PointsController.cs:100), so the method returns nothing and the caller re-reads its own points to pick up the new opt-in state and display name.
      • GetPointsOverviewAsync (:82-99) GETs points/overview?recentCount= (:88) for the organizer rollup.
      • All four share the same three-step failure handling: retry with the cancellation-token overload, ServiceExceptionHelper translation on a non-success, then EnsureSuccessStatusCode().
    • Why it's built this way: every number the game shows (totals, per-activity breakdowns, ranks) is computed server-side and arrives as a DTO, so there is no client-side scoring logic to keep in step with the earn rules. [Rubric §30, Compliance, Privacy & Data Governance]: the organizer rollup deliberately carries activity, points and timestamps but no attendee identity (PointsController.cs:117-118), so the widest-reading call in this class is also the one that returns the least personal data. See ADR-072 for the surrounding decision.
    • Where it's used: registered scoped as IPointsUIService (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:44); consumed by MyPoints (MyPoints.razor.cs:74, :82, :108) and OrganizerPointsOverview (OrganizerPointsOverview.razor.cs:45). The whole controller sits behind the EngagementFeatures.Points gate (PointsController.cs:34, see EngagementFeatures and ADR-031).
    • Caveats / not-in-source: there is no PointsServiceTests file; coverage comes through the bUnit page tests, which mock IPointsUIService. How points are earned and what each activity is worth is server-side rule, nothing in this file.

    AttendeeSummary

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/IAttendeeLookupService.cs:13 · Level 5 · record

    • What it is: the subset of an attendee's account the check-in surfaces need, five positional members plus one computed display name. Enough to recognise a person standing at a door, and nothing more (IAttendeeLookupService.cs:13-32).
    • Depends on: the UserIdentifierType alias and four strings. Nothing first-party; notably not Identity's UserListDTO.
    • Concept introduced, the consumer-owned view contract. [Rubric §7, Microservices Readiness] assesses whether a module's cross-boundary dependencies are on contracts it can keep working when the other side becomes a separate service. The doc comment (:5-7) states the decision outright: this record is declared here rather than reusing the Identity list DTO, so the Engagement UI depends on the Identity HTTP contract only, not on the Identity assembly. [Rubric §30, Compliance, Privacy & Data Governance] is the second reason to declare it narrowly: the Identity users endpoint can return more about a person than a door scanner needs, and a hand-declared five-field record is a data-minimisation decision expressed in the type system. The same instinct appears one level down in the private AttendeeRow (AttendeeLookupService.cs:170-175), whose comment says only the fields the check-in surfaces render are read.
    • Walkthrough: the positional members are UserId, Email, FirstName, LastName and Role (:14-18). DisplayName (:24-31) is the only behavior: it trims "{FirstName} {LastName}" and falls back to Email when the result is blank, with the comment giving the reason, a row must never render empty. That single fallback is why a caller can bind a grid column straight to DisplayName without a null guard. The sibling enum in the same file, AttendeeSearchField (:39), names which field a single search term applies to.
    • Why it's built this way: a record (not a class) gives value equality for free, which matters because the search panel re-fetches pages and compares rows. It stays mutable-free through positional init members, in line with the workspace's required/init default.
    • Where it's used: the grid item type of AttendeeSearchPanel (AttendeeSearchPanel.razor.cs:16, with the filter columns named through nameof(AttendeeSummary.Email) and friends at :80-82, :114-117), the payload of its OnCheckIn callback (:27, :130), and the argument of CheckInScan's manual check-in path (CheckInScan.razor.cs:173). Produced only by AttendeeLookupService.ToSummary (AttendeeLookupService.cs:163-164).
    • Caveats / not-in-source: Role is a plain string mirroring whatever the Identity endpoint sends; no enum or constant in this file constrains it.

    IAttendeeLookupService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/IAttendeeLookupService.cs:55 · Level 6 · interface

    • What it is: the cross-module lookup contract over the Identity users endpoint, used by the organizer check-in fallback to find an attendee by name or email and to put a name on the person a scan just resolved (:51-54). Two members, deliberately asymmetric.
    • Depends on: AttendeeSummary (the row shape), the UserIdentifierType alias.
    • Concept introduced, a contract shaped by what the server can actually answer. [Rubric §9, API & Contract Design] assesses whether an abstraction reflects the capability underneath it rather than an idealised one. The obvious contract here would be GetByIdAsync, and it is precisely the one the Identity endpoint cannot serve: it filters by name and email but takes no identifier (see the implementation's class doc, AttendeeLookupService.cs:11-13). So the interface exposes what the transport supports, a filtered paged SearchAsync, plus a narrower GetDisplayNameAsync whose implementation is free to solve the identifier problem however it can. [Rubric §1, SOLID]: the two members are the two questions the check-in surfaces ask, and the second returns a string? rather than a full record because a scan only needs a name to render.
    • Walkthrough
      • SearchAsync (:69-77) takes three optional filter fragments (email, firstName, lastName), paging (pageNumber = 1, pageSize = 10), optional sortColumn / sortDirection, and a token. It returns (IReadOnlyList<AttendeeSummary> Items, int TotalItems): the tuple exists because a data grid needs the total to draw a pager, not just the page. The doc comment (:57-59) names the matching semantics: each filter is a "contains" match, and the server ANDs them. That is the fact the sibling enum AttendeeSearchField exists to work around (:34-38): one search box holding one term has to say which field it means, because broadcasting the term across all three ANDed filters would match nothing.
      • GetDisplayNameAsync (:85-87) resolves one attendee's display name by identifier, or null when the account is unknown, and its comment says where that identifier typically comes from: a scan (:83).
    • Where it's used: implemented by AttendeeLookupService, registered scoped (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:48). Injected by AttendeeSearchPanel (AttendeeSearchPanel.razor.cs:21) for the search half and by CheckInScan (CheckInScan.razor.cs:22, used at :220) for the name resolution half.
    • Caveats / not-in-source: the interface says nothing about caching or freshness; the roster snapshot and its recheck window are entirely an implementation decision, described at AttendeeLookupService.

    AttendeeLookupService

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI.Services · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/Services/AttendeeLookupService.cs:16 · Level 7 · sealed class

    • What it is: the HTTP implementation of IAttendeeLookupService over the Identity /Users endpoint. Search reads straight through to the API; the by-identifier lookup is answered from an in-memory roster snapshot this class builds and refreshes itself, because the endpoint cannot be queried by identifier (class doc, :9-15).

    • Depends on: IAttendeeLookupService, AttendeeSummary, AuthenticatedServiceBase, ITokenStorageService, ServiceExceptionHelper, PagedCollectionResult<T>. Externals: IHttpClientFactory, TimeProvider, System.Net.Http.Json, System.Globalization.

    • Concept introduced, the scoped roster snapshot with single-flight and a negative-cache window. This is the most stateful class in the group, and each piece of that state answers a specific failure of the naive version. [Rubric §12, Performance & Scalability] assesses whether repeated work is avoided on a hot path, and the hot path here is real: an organizer scanning badges at a door produces a stream of identifier lookups, many of them for codes the roster does not know. The naive implementation refetched the entire roster on every unrecognised code, which the comment at :111-113 records as the defect this design fixes. Three mechanisms combine:

      1. The snapshot (_rosterNames, :41), an identifier-to-name map built once per scanning session.
      2. Single flight (_rosterLoad, :44, documented as "published so concurrent misses share it instead of stampeding"), so several misses arriving together await one load.
      3. A negative-cache window (RosterRecheckInterval, 60 seconds, :37), so a miss against a fresh snapshot is reported as a genuine unknown instead of triggering a refetch, while a miss against a stale one refetches and lets an attendee who registered mid-conference resolve on the next scan.

      [Rubric §14, Testability] shows in the constructor: TimeProvider? timeProvider = null (:19) defaulting to TimeProvider.System (:39) is what makes the 60-second window testable without a real 60-second wait, and AttendeeLookupServiceTests uses exactly that (:50). [Rubric §19, State Management] covers the lifetime question: the service is registered scoped (MMCA.ADC.Engagement.UI/DependencyInjection.cs:48), so the snapshot lives and dies with the Blazor circuit rather than being a process-wide cache of attendee names.

    • Walkthrough (constants, then the two public members, then the loader)

      • The constants encode the sizing decisions: RosterPageSize = 500 (:24, matching the server's page cap noted as BR-11), MaxRosterPages = 5 (:30), and the comment there gives both reasons for the bound, five pages cover 2500 accounts (far above the real attendee count) and a paging bug therefore cannot loop forever.
      • SearchAsync (:49-90) assembles its query into a Dictionary<string, string?> with an ordinal comparer (:59-68), then drops blank values and URL-escapes the rest in one LINQ pass (:70-72). That is the notable difference from the hand-built query strings elsewhere in the module: filters here are genuinely optional, so "omit when empty" has to be a rule rather than a set of if statements. The response is read as a PagedCollectionResult<AttendeeRow> (:86) and each row is projected to an AttendeeSummary via ToSummary (:88, :163-164), returning the page plus PaginationMetadata.TotalItemCount (:89).
      • GetDisplayNameAsync (:93-130) is the interesting one, and it reads as four ordered guards. A snapshot hit returns immediately (:97-100). Otherwise, if a load is already in flight, the caller awaits that task and reads its result rather than starting a second one (:102-106). Otherwise, if the snapshot exists and is younger than RosterRecheckInterval measured against the injected clock, the miss is answered null (:108-114). Only past that does it start a load, publish it to _rosterLoad (:116-117), await it, and stamp _rosterLoadedAtTicks (:120-121). The finally (:124-127) clears _rosterLoad even on failure, with the comment giving the reason: a failed load must leave the next miss free to retry rather than be told "recently loaded".
      • LoadRosterAsync (:136-161) pages the roster with SearchAsync itself, no filters, 500 rows per page, sorted by Email ascending (:142-147). The sort is not cosmetic: it is what makes page boundaries stable across the several requests that make up one snapshot, so a row cannot be skipped by shifting between calls. The loop stops early when a page comes back empty or when the accumulated count reaches the server's reported total (:154-157).
      • AttendeeRow (:170-175) is the private wire shape, deliberately narrower than what the endpoint returns (:166-169).

    • Why it's built this way: the class doc (:11-15) frames it as mirroring SessionLookupService, search straight through, by-identifier from a snapshot, with a miss refreshing the snapshot once. The design is driven entirely by a server capability gap rather than by a performance target, and the constants are sized for one conference (about 2500 accounts, at a real observed attendance far below that), not for a general-purpose directory.

    • Where it's used: AttendeeSearchPanel drives SearchAsync from both its data-grid loader (AttendeeSearchPanel.razor.cs:79) and its mobile infinite-scroll loader (:94); CheckInScan calls GetDisplayNameAsync after a scan resolves to an identifier (CheckInScan.razor.cs:220). [Rubric §14, Testability]: four facts in AttendeeLookupServiceTests cover exactly the four behaviors above, one roster fetch for repeated unknown ids (:19), a known id answered from the snapshot (:35), a refetch past the recheck interval that resolves a new attendee (:50), and concurrent misses sharing one load (:72).

    • Caveats / not-in-source: three things are worth knowing before relying on this class.

      • The /Users endpoint is permission-gated on IdentityPermissions.UsersRead (MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.API/Controllers/UsersController.cs:125, documented there as requiring the Organizer role, BR-51), so a non-organizer caller gets a failure translated by ServiceExceptionHelper, not an empty list.
      • MaxRosterPages * RosterPageSize is a hard 2500-account ceiling on the snapshot. Past it, ids on later pages would never resolve by name, and nothing logs or surfaces that.
      • The single-flight guarantee rests on _rosterLoad being assigned (:117) before any yield point, with no lock taken and the field neither volatile nor interlocked. On the single-threaded Blazor circuit context that holds, and the concurrency test exercises it, but the class is not written to be shared across threads and its scoped registration is what keeps it from being.

    CreateBookmarkRequestValidator

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application.UserSessionBookmarks.UseCases.Create · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application/UserSessionBookmarks/UseCases/Create/CreateBookmarkRequestValidator.cs:9 · Level 1 · class (sealed)

    • What it is: the shape gate in front of CreateBookmarkHandler. Two rules, both saying the same thing about a different identifier: a bookmark needs a real user and a real session.
    • Depends on: CreateBookmarkRequest, the UserIdentifierType / SessionIdentifierType aliases; externals: FluentValidation (AbstractValidator<T>, RuleFor, NotEqual, WithErrorCode).
    • Concept introduced, the request validator and where it sits in the command pipeline. [Rubric §9, API & Contract Design] (assesses whether a contract states its own preconditions and reports violations in a machine-readable form) and [Rubric §5, Vertical Slice] (assesses whether everything one use case needs lives in the use case's own folder). The wiring is worth learning once because every validator in this unit rides it. Validators are never resolved by name: ScanModuleApplicationServices<ClassReference>() in the module's composition root (MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application/DependencyInjection.cs:82) scans the assembly and registers each AbstractValidator<T> as IValidator<T>. At request time ValidatingCommandDecorator<TCommand, TResult> receives IEnumerable<IValidator<TCommand>> and keeps the first one (MMCA.Common/Source/Core/MMCA.Common.Application/UseCases/Decorators/ValidatingCommandDecorator.cs:26,29), so the validation runs before the handler ever sees the command, and a handler is free to assume the structural rules already held. The other thing to notice is WithErrorCode: the string "UserSessionBookmark.UserId.Required" (CreateBookmarkRequestValidator.cs:16) is a stable code a client can branch on, which a localized message never is.
    • Walkthrough: a block-bodied constructor with two rules (CreateBookmarkRequestValidator.cs:11-22).
      • UserId must not equal default(UserIdentifierType) (CreateBookmarkRequestValidator.cs:13-16). The alias is int (MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Identity/MMCA.ADC.Identity.Shared/MMCA.ADC.Identity.GlobalUsings.IdentifierType.cs:2), so this rejects an unset zero. Writing it as NotEqual(default(...)) rather than GreaterThan(0) keeps the rule correct if the alias ever changes width or becomes a struct id.
      • SessionId gets the identical treatment against default(SessionIdentifierType) (CreateBookmarkRequestValidator.cs:18-21), also an int alias (MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/MMCA.ADC.Conference.GlobalUsings.IdentifierType.cs:14).
    • Why it's built this way: the validator deliberately checks only what it can decide without touching a database or another module. Ownership (may this caller bookmark for this user) is enforced in BookmarksController against the caller's user_id claim (MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.API/Controllers/BookmarksController.cs:56-63, ADR-033), and session eligibility (BR-49, BR-91) is a cross-module call inside the handler. Three checks, three layers, none duplicated.
    • Where it's used: applied automatically to every CreateBookmarkRequest command entering the pipeline; the request itself arrives from BookmarksController.CreateAsync (BookmarksController.cs:47-65).

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application.CheckIns.UseCases.RecordRoomCheckIn · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application/CheckIns/UseCases/RecordRoomCheckIn/RoomCheckInRequestValidator.cs:9 · Level 1 · class (sealed)

    • What it is: the one-rule validator for the room-QR self check-in request. The room id must be a positive number, and that is the entire structural contract of the request.
    • Depends on: RoomCheckInRequest, the RoomIdentifierType alias; externals: FluentValidation.
    • Concept reinforced: the pipeline wiring taught under CreateBookmarkRequestValidator. [Rubric §11, Security] is the interesting tag here, in the negative: the request is this thin on purpose. The attendee names a room and nothing else, and the server resolves which session that room is hosting, so there is no field a caller could tamper with to check in somewhere they are not standing (see RecordRoomCheckInHandler).
    • Walkthrough: an expression-bodied constructor holding a single RuleFor (RoomCheckInRequestValidator.cs:11-15): RoomId GreaterThan(0), message "Room ID is required.", error code "CheckIn.RoomId.Required". GreaterThan(0) works because RoomIdentifierType is int (MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Conference/MMCA.ADC.Conference.Shared/MMCA.ADC.Conference.GlobalUsings.IdentifierType.cs:11).

    MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application · MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application.CheckIns.UseCases.RecordSponsorVisit · MMCA.ADC/Source/Modules/Engagement/MMCA.ADC.Engagement.Application/CheckIns/UseCases/RecordSponsorVisit/SponsorVisitRequestValidator.cs:9 · Level 1 · class (sealed)

The error code prefix is CheckIn. rather than Sponsor. because a booth visit is stored as a CheckIn row with Scope = Sponsor, and the code namespace follows the aggregate, not the endpoint.
